市場焦點
美國
• 9月CPI年增3.7%、PPI年增6.3%，雙雙高於前期 ，能源價格的急升是通膨回升的主要推力 。
雖然核心CPI年增率僅升至2.6%，但住房與服務價格仍維持黏著，使通膨降溫過程出現反覆；
同時PPI增幅明顯高於 CPI，反映企業成本壓力偏高 ，未來不排除進一步向終端價格傳導 。整
體而言，CPI與PPI同步回升反映通膨降溫進程仍存在不確定性 ，進而令市場重估通膨回落速
度與對Fed貨幣政策路徑的審慎預期。
AI
• AI雲端業者 CoreWeave的5年期CDS利差一度突破 1,140個基點 ，旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近
15%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延至股票市場 。全球最大半導體設備商ASML表示，客戶已開始洽
談2029至2030年的訂單；晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期 。與此同時，台灣出口增

速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季
相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。
中國
• 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力
仍在，彭博表示以GDP平減指數衡量 ，經歷三年下滑後 ，整體經濟預計將在 2026年走出通縮 ，
投資上短期可聚焦，受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。

美股獲利引擎轉向半導體/核心AI與能源
• 雖然標普500指數在2026年第三季展現出獲利韌性 ，但隨著油價上漲侵蝕利潤率 、消費者受通膨壓抑 ，以及分析師下調未
來預測動能，市場未來的容錯空間正在縮小。
• AI與半導體仍為獲利領頭羊：科技與半導體持續為標普500指數獲利成長的核心動力，其中約20家企業組成的核心AI一籃子
股票(權重約34.6%)對指數獲利的貢獻度已超過科技七雄(Mag 7)，且此優勢預計將至少延續至2028年底。
• 油價推升能源產業，接棒成為強棒：受到近期國際油價上揚影響，能源產業獲利預測顯著上修，預計獲利成長勁道將延續至
2027年初。在接下來的三個季度中，能源板塊的收益成長表現預計將超越科技七雄。
• 消費與運輸業面臨成本壓頂：相較於科技與能源的強勁，必需消費品與交通運輸業正承受顯著壓力。例如，達美航空受高燃

料成本拖累，而Nike與百事可樂則反映出消費者因持續通膨而縮減支出的困境。
7月以來EPS和營收成長預期修正
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
剔除能源、科技七雄和AI一籃子股票後的指數收益成長

利潤率高檔盤整、關稅退款帶來短補，但動能已見放緩
• 利潤率高檔盤整，淨利潤率提升受限：標普500成分股第二季營運利潤率(18.7%)與淨利潤率(15.6%)均創下新高。雖然第三
季營運利潤率預估可進一步提升至19.4%，但淨利潤率預計微幅上升至15.8%，顯示整體企業淨利潤的擴張動能正漸趨平緩 。
• 700億美元關稅退款提供短線補藥： 受惠於美國政府依據國際緊急經濟權力法 (IEEPA)的追償退稅，估計仍有至少700億美元
的關稅款將退還給標普 500成分股(主要集中於工業、非必需消費及醫療保健等 )，這預計能為2026年標普500指數每股收益
(EPS)成長帶來約235個基點、利潤率提升約34個基點的短期提振。
• 上修動能開始轉弱：雖然整體財報指引動量仍維持正值，但過去六週內，分析師對未來12個月EPS與收入的修正動能出現頻

繁下修，這意味著市場先前的極度樂觀情緒正在消退，未來財報季度要優於預期的門檻正在提高。
• 小結與投資建議： 標普500指數短期內靠著半導體 /AI與能源雙引擎拉抬 ，加上關稅退款的一次性利多注入 ，下半年整體獲
利依然具備支撐。然而，高油價帶來的營運成本上升 ，以及終端消費力道的疲軟 ，正逐漸侵蝕非科技/非能源企業的利潤 ，
而大盤廣度不足(僅少數板塊支撐獲利)，代表未來投資風險正逐漸集中 ，但AI算力需求仍屬硬性需求，但波動加劇下，投資
建議以定期定額方式投資美股核心AI與半導體龍頭股票。
利潤率預測
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
各產業第二季度收到的關稅退款
未來12月EPS預期修正動能與營收

顧
美國9月CPI、PPI 同步升溫，能源衝擊擴大通膨與利率風險
• 能源價格急升，消費通膨再升溫：美國9月CPI月增0.6%、
年增3.7%，高於前月 3.4%，其中汽油月增 9.1%、能源
價格月增 5.2%，汽油單項即貢獻整體 CPI當月漲幅逾半；
另一方面，核心CPI月增0.3%、年增2.6%、住房指數月
增0.4%，顯示能源雖是本次通膨回升主因 ，但住房及部
分服務價格仍具黏著性，令通膨降溫進程面臨反覆。
• 上游價格大幅攀升 ，成本傳導風險升高： 9月PPI最終需
求月增1.3%、年增6.3%，創2022年3月以來最大增幅，
其中最終需求商品月增 2.6%，能源價格大漲 10.8%，汽
油價格更跳升 20.3%；核心PPI月增0.5%、年增5.0%，
顯示價格壓力不僅來自能源 ，也涉及部分商品與服務環

節；後續是否進一步推升至 CPI，仍須觀察企業成本轉
嫁程度及需求變化。
• 市場看法： 通膨回升恐提高 Fed再度升息機率 ，並延長
高利率維持時間 ，甚至不排除進一步緊縮的可能 ，短期
有利支撐美債殖利率及美元表現 ，不過股、債市後續走
勢仍將受到貨幣政策預期與風險情緒變化影響 。資產配
置建議可關注具成本轉嫁能力 、現金流穩健的能源類股；
債券則宜以短中天期 、高信用評等標的為主 ，以降低利
率敏感度。黃金可作為分散地緣政治及通膨風險的輔助
配置，但不宜將任何單一資產視為必然受惠標的。
CPI 與核心 CPI 走勢變動

顧
供給面推動中國9月CPI升0.9%
• 供給面推動中國9月通膨復甦：中國9月通膨數據顯示，CPI年增率由8月的0.8%升至0.9%，連續兩個月擴大，但低於市場預期
的1.0%，物價上漲主要受到能源及旅遊價格帶動，而食品價格跌幅進一步擴大，使整體通膨壓力仍相對溫和，扣除食品與能源
後，核心CPI維持在1.0%，反映內需需求尚未明顯升溫 。另一方面，PPI年增率由3.8%大幅升至5.0%，優於市場預期，且月增
率達1.2%，創4月以來最大漲幅，顯示上游原物料及工業品價格回升，須關注未來是否逐步傳導至消費端價格。
• 展望後市： 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求 ，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力仍 在，彭博表示 以
GDP平減指數衡量，經歷三年下滑後，整體經濟預計將在2026年走出通縮，值得注意的是，這種供給面驅動的通膨復甦，將降

低官方推出需求刺激措施的緊迫感 ，而要反轉通縮循環正需要官方舉措，投資上短期可聚焦受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的
半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.14
通膨集中於能源和AI相關行業

顧
過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數
的兩倍
• 過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數的兩倍：主因為獲利帶動成長，整體而言，新興市場企
業獲利表現強勁 ，而且仍持續加速成長 ，高盛預估2026年新興市場企業盈餘成長率將達 55%，超過標普500企業盈餘成長速
度的兩倍，且新興市場股票估值較美國股市存在超過40%的折價空間，評價相對具吸引力，今年以來流入新興市場股市的資金
已接近500億美元，創下過去20年來最強勁紀錄之一。更重要的是新興市場國家目前貢獻了全球近 80%的AI硬體出口。隨著AI
產業持續發展，受惠範圍有望從少數龍頭企業擴大至更多國家及公司，特別是那些能夠協助解決供應鏈瓶頸的企業。
• 結論：後市來看在AI產業趨勢、相對低估的評價水準，以及強勁企業獲利成長三大因素支撐下，新興市場股票未來一年仍具備

進一步上漲的潛力，有機會延續目前的強勢表現。
MSCI新興市場股票表現亮眼

AI投資支撐美國成長，融資需求升高使長端利率與信用風險並存
• AI基礎建設持續擴張 ，成為企業投資重要動能 ：大型雲端業者
持續投入資料中心 、半導體、網路設備與電力基礎設施 ，帶動
相關產業資本支出與供應鏈需求 。摩根大通資產管理指出 ，納
入資料中心開發商及半導體投資後，AI 基礎建設至 2030 年的
累計投資需求可能達約 5兆美元 ，顯示AI投資循環仍具規模 ，
但實際經濟貢獻與企業獲利 ，仍取決於投資效率及商業化成果 。
• 外部融資需求增加 ，債券供給與投資回報成關鍵： 隨AI投資規
模擴大，大型科技企業逐步增加公司債與外部融資 ，在企業債
與公債供給同步增加下 ，長天期利率面臨上行壓力 。若未來AI
獲利成長未能跟上資本支出擴張速度 ，可能壓縮自由現金流與

資本報酬率，進一步擴大信用利差 ，並提高高估值科技股的評
價修正風險
• 市場觀察：AI基礎建設仍是未來數年推動全球科技投資與企業
數位轉型的成長主軸，相關需求具備中長期發展空間，而2026
年市場已逐漸轉向檢視投資回報率 、現金流創造能力及融資結
構是否具備可持續性 ，因此單純依賴資本支出擴張已不足以支
撐所有AI概念股評價 。建議配置可兼顧具獲利與現金流支撐的
AI供應鏈，以及高信用評等 、中短天期債券；對長天期債券則
須評估供給與利率風險 ，並審慎檢視高負債 AI企業的償債能力
及投資回報。
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，永豐金證券， 2026.10.12

AI熱潮、高債務與網路威脅，IMF示警三大金融穩定風險升高
資料來源：Bloomberg Finance L.P， Dealogic，Zero day Clock and IMF staff calculations.， 2026.10.13
AI相關風險持續升溫 主權債券殖利率攀升 網路資安威脅升高
大型雲端科技企業債券發行規模
（占投資等級公司債市場比重) 10年期公債殖利率平均變動幅度 零時差漏洞占比（%）
• AI估值高度集中，市場重定價風險升溫：IMF於10月《全球金融穩定報告》指出，AI相關投資已成為全球成長的重要支撐，
但資金與漲勢高度集中於少數企業及市場，一旦獲利成長不如預期或估值遭重新檢視，可能引發科技股修正並透過風險偏好
下降擴散至整體金融市場。
• 通膨與財政疑慮恐推升債券市場波動： IMF認為，若能源價格推升通膨壓力或市場對財政永續性產生疑慮 ，主權債殖利率可

能進一步上升，進而提高全球融資成本與資產重估風險；在高利率與高債務環境下，金融條件快速緊縮的情況將明顯提高。
• 網路安全風險成為潛在威脅： 隨金融體系數位化程度提高 ，IMF將大型網路攻擊列為重要風險來源之一 。若攻擊事件波及支
付系統、金融基礎設施或雲端服務平台，不僅可能影響市場運作效率，也可能透過信心衝擊與流動性壓力擴大金融市場波動。
• 投資建議：市場成長動能仍受AI支撐，但在金融穩定風險上升情境下，建議採取「AI受惠股＋高品質債券＋防禦型資產」的
槓鈴策略；核心配置可聚焦全球AI基礎建設供應鏈及投資等級債，藉由企業長期獲利成長與債券避險功能，同時因應估值修
正及金融條件收緊風險。

AI隱憂浮現但動能仍在
• CoreWeave信用風險亮紅燈：市場憂心雲端業者在算力租賃業務產生足夠回報前，恐難以負擔龐大債務，導致信用風險升
溫。上週，AI雲端業者CoreWeave的5年期信用違約交換（CDS）利差一度突破1,140個基點，升至通常反映嚴重信用壓力
的水準；旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近15%，其中2032年到期的無擔保債券於上週三跌破面值的80%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延
至股票市場。
• AI半導體產業的基本面仍具韌性：全球最大半導體設備商艾司摩爾（ASML）表示，客戶已開始洽談2029至2030年的訂單；
晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期。與此同時，台灣出口增速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯
示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。

美銀美林調查指出，全球經理人對景氣與股市仍維持偏多立場
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
• 經理人樂觀情緒維持高檔： 美銀美林10月全球基金經理人調查顯示 ，市場風險偏好仍高 ，平均現金水位維持在 3.9%的低檔，
科技股配置則升至2020年8月以來最高，反映經理人對AI產業及企業獲利前景仍具信心 。然而，整體經濟成長預期已轉趨保
守，淨4%的受訪者預期未來 12個月經濟成長放緩 。極端風險方面，債券殖利率無序攀升仍居首位 ，占比31%；AI泡沫及通
膨再起分別為23%及20%。此外，39%的經理人認為，大型雲端業者資本支出是系統性信用事件的主要潛在來源 ，顯示市場
焦點已從AI需求成長延伸至龐大資本支出的獲利回收能力。

• 美國公債殖利率走高 ，債券的收益吸引力逐漸回升： 調查顯示，37%的受訪者表示，當美國10年期公債殖利率升至 6%時，
才會考慮由股票轉向債券；另有19%認為目前殖利率已具吸引力，顯示部分經理人已開始重新評估固定收益資產的配置價值 。
不過，債券整體仍處淨減碼，且經理人對科技股的配置持續升高 ，反映市場尚未全面轉向防禦。後續仍須關注美債殖利率變
化、AI資本支出的投資回報，以及企業獲利能否支撐偏高的市場評價。
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10

台積電財報佳卻未激勵股價，短線面臨總經干擾
• 台積電Q3營收、獲利及EPS同步改寫單季新高：營收年增50.9%、季增17.6%，稅後純益7,630.6億元，年增68.7％、季增8
％；每股稅後純益(EPS)29.43元，整體優於市場預期，但毛利率66.4％較第二季的67.7％下降。
• 財測展望：第四季財測營收預估 523~543億美元，季增上看15％、年增65％，毛利率預估65%~67%，反映高階晶片需求
依然強勁。全年展望方面，預期2026年美元營收年增約44%，較上季預估的「略高於40%」說法更加明確。此外，重申全
年資本支出600~640億美元不變。
• 市場反應：台積電法說會隔日股價小幅下跌，法說會未出現激勵行情，主要原因包含美國通膨升溫壓力、外資連續調節部位，

以及毛利率略為下降。短線籌碼面可能仍會調整，但台積電董事長魏哲家在法說會中強調，第二波AI需求正強勁興起，台積
電正透過多種途徑全力擴充產能，顯示高階晶片前景明確，中長期具投資價值，多家投資機構也紛紛上調目標價。
資料來源：台積電法說會，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.15。
法說會重點整理 說明
AI需求持續升溫
魏哲家坦言，目前仍難以判斷晶圓供需缺口將擴大或縮小，因為
客戶需求持續增加，台積電也正積極提高產能。儘管消費性電子
市場仍受到零組件漲價及總體經濟不確定性影響，但先進製程需
求依然強勁，公司預估2026年全年美元營收將年增約44％。
擴廠進度
(1)台南科學園區新建3奈米廠預計2027年下半年量產，並決定在
台南再增建一座3奈米廠，規畫2028年投入量產。
(2)美國亞利桑那州第二座晶圓廠已完成廠房興建，預計2027年
第三季量產，時程較原先規畫略為提前；日本第二座晶圓廠則將
導入3奈米製程，預計2028年量產。
台積電證實洽談Terafab
合作
魏哲家強調，目前相關合作仍在討論階段，尚未有結論。不過，

無論未來採取何種合作模式，晶圓廠都必須由台積電負責營運。
電力供應疑慮
美國電力供應情況是公司評估AI長期需求的重要因素之一，因此
台積電進一步確認當地電力供應狀況，目前相關研究及評估結果
均良好，尚未發現明顯問題。

• 全球經濟正處於「韌性仍在、風險升高」的階段：國際貨幣基金組織(IMF)指出，AI投資熱潮持續推動美國、台灣等國家成
長，但中東地緣政治衝突、高通膨風險、政府債務問題與AI泡沫化疑慮，也同時為市場埋下不確定因素。
• 市場永遠充滿雜音，定期定額紀律投資更具優勢：本許多人認為定期定額的核心在於「平均成本」，但更重要的是幫助投資
人克服人性。市場上漲時持續投入，可以參與企業獲利成長帶來的成果，市場下跌時持續扣款，則能以較低價格累積更多單
位數。換句話說，定期定額讓投資人不需要每天判斷：明天會不會跌？現在是不是太貴？要不要先停扣觀望？長期而言，紀

律投資往往比短期擇時的成效更佳。
• AI時代更適合定期定額：儘管IMF認為人工智慧發展為全球經濟成長帶來貢獻，但同時也警告，若市場對AI未來獲利能力過
度樂觀，不排除出現股市估值修正。根據歷史經驗，每一次市場重大修正往往也孕育下一輪成長契機，不論AI發展是否出現
泡沫，善用定期定額策略可以在機會與風險之間達到平衡。
韌性仍在、風險升溫，紀律投資更穩健
任一月份定期定額進場投資試算
註：以MSCI全球指數2010/1/31~2025/12/31試算。假設定期定額每月月底投入、
原幣計算。指數資料試算僅供參考，非投資商品之實際績效。資料來源Bloomberg。
短進短出錯失行情的代價大
註：以美國標普500指數為試算範例，期間為1980年~2025年。資料來源Bloomberg。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide our latest views on Delta after its 3Q26 results 
call on 29 October.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. During the results call, Delta 
remained positive on AI monetisation after its analysis of Anthropic’s S-1 
filing. Management flagged shortages of components, power and skilled 
electricians and slower-than-expected data centre buildout to be potential 
hiccups in the process; however, it still sees strong underlying demand. As 
such, we still expect sequential revenue growth in both 4Q26E and 1Q27E 
as component shortages had deferred 3Q26 demand. On rising material 
costs, Delta noted that BoM costs have increased by 2% as of 3Q26 (vs. 
early 2026), with the company gradually passing these costs through to 
end customers in 4Q26 and 1Q27. We believe the main gross margin 
driver will likely still be the absence of inventory write-offs and a higher 
power mix in 4Q26E. We forecast a gross margin recovery to 36% and a 
record operating margin of 18.5% in 4Q26. As for long-term growth, we 
maintain our investment theses that DC-DC module and HVDC adoption 
will become key growth drivers for Delta in 2027 and 2028, respectively.

3Q26 results missed on gross margin. Delta's 3Q26 earnings were 
TWD23.5bn (EPS: TWD9.04), missing our estimate of TWD27.3bn and 
Bloomberg consensus estimate of TWD29.6bn by 14% and 21%, 
respectively. We attribute the weakness mainly to lower-than-expected 
gross margin, driven by: 1) higher-than-expected inventory write-offs amid 
product shipment hiccups; 2) a one-off provision for customer claims, with 
the related loss likely to ultimately be borne by the supplier; and 3) rising 
material costs. Based on our calculation, Delta recognised c.TWD4bn of 
inventory provision loss in 3Q26, which reduced gross margin by 2pp. 
Excluding inventory provision loss, gross margin was c.35.8%, close to our 
previous estimate of 36.3%. 3Q26 revenue reached TWD197.1bn (up 8% 
QoQ and 31% YoY), which was also negatively affected by product 
shipment hiccups (vs. our previous estimate of 10% QoQ growth).

What we recommend: We trim our 2026-28E EPS by 2-6% to reflect lower 
revenue assumptions due to the impact of component shortages. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD3,000 (from 
TWD3,088) based on an unchanged target PER of 49x (high-end of its 3-
year range of 11-50x) applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2028E revenue is 12% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish assumption regarding HVDC 
adoption.

29 October 2026

3Q26 review: margin likely bottomed-out

➢ Gross margin to recover on lower inventory provisions in 4Q26 
➢ Addressable market remains intact despite some near-term hiccups

Company profile 
Founded in 1971, Delta Electronics has been the global leader in switching power supply solutions since 
2002 and DC brushless fans since 2006. Its business covers 3 key areas: power electronics (eg, power 
supplies, thermal management products and passive components), automation (eg, controllers, robot 
arms and building management systems), and infrastructure (eg, datacentre infrastructure, networking 
and EV charging).

### Page 5

5

Delta Electronics (2308 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 30 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Delta's board has 7 non-independent and 5 independent directors with a term of three years.
The total number of directors and ratio of independent directors appear higher than most other
listed companies. Besides, according to Delta's CSR report, enhancing the board's functions is
one of the key goals for the company to achieve better corporate governance; also, the board
members' diversity and professionalism will be taken into consideration to ensure a good board
quality. Moreover, the company evaluates all the directors in the board using internal regulations
and an external independent institution each year and at least once every three years,
respectively. We believe Delta has implemented relatively more enhanced measure than most
listed firms to improve its board quality.
Capital management 2
During 2016-19, Delta's payout ratio was stable in the range of c.70%, but fell to 56% in 2020
and 53% in 2021. For 2020, it was mainly due to a sizeable non-cash re-valuation gain after the
acquisition of Delta Thailand (DET), while the company maintained more cash on hand to deal
with the uncertainties caused by COVID-19. However, the DPS growth from 2016 to 2021 was
limited, as Delta struggled with business diversification into new end-markets (eg, smartphone,
NEV). We believe that Delta's effort started to bear fruit in recent years after its NEV business
has gradually picked up. Overall, we see a stable dividend policy from Delta and expect its DPS
to continue to grow YoY in the future given that they declared a TWD11.6 DPS for 2025 which
equals a 50% payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1 Revenue from related parties stood at 12% of total 2025 revenue and purchases from related parties were at 11%. We see limited risk from related party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
Delta focuses on: 1) localised management, 2) supplier certification and risk management, and
3) supplier sustainability engagement as key initiatives under the scope of supply chain
management. In 2019, 144 new suppliers joined Delta's supply chain system. All of them passed
environmental and social evaluations, signed the declaration of "non-use of conflict materials",
and complied with Responsible Business Alliance's (BRA) Code of Conduct. Also, these vendors
had to implement and abide by Delta's supply chain CSR policy and code of conduct. To lower
ESG risks, Delta carries out RBA audits and monitors high-risk critical suppliers each year. The
suppliers that fail these audits have to provide detailed improvement plans in response to
identified issues. Besides, Delta has surveyed a total of 263 suppliers with potential risk and 63
suppliers with high risk of the total 1,331 suppliers, based on its CSR policy and code of conduct.
If these suppliers are flagged as high-risk and no concrete improvements are made, Delta may
consider terminating cooperation and transactions with them to improve the company's
engagement on supplier sustainability. Overall, we think Delta has a solid plan to improve its
supply chain management with specific KPIs, including: 1) achieving supplier improvement rate
of 80% in 2025, 2) reducing GHG emissions rate of critical suppliers by 10% in 2025, and 3)
improving water saving rate of critical suppliers by 12% in 2025.
S Data security 2
Delta's Information Security team created Delta's Information Security Policy in November 2013
and started providing educational training to global employees in 2014. Delta gradually
introduced the Information Security Management System (ISMS) in 2016 and obtained the ISO
27001 international certification in 2018. According to ISO27001, Delta has to conduct at least
one internal audit on information security and appoint an independent institution to conduct audit
externally. According to Delta, no major deficiencies have been found in recent years.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

3Q26 miss on GM: See details in Results Summary
Growth momentum seen sustained into 4Q26 and 2027: Management indicated
that the strong business momentum observed in 3Q26 is likely to stay intact going

forward, driven by both power testing and semi & photonics businesses. Normalized
GM remains at ~60% (2026 guidance of ~60% GM implies GM back to ~60% in
4Q26 vs. 57.5% in 3Q26).
• Power testing: 60-70% of revenue is AI-related for 2026, and it is expected
to be sustained into 2027. HVDC testing tools could be the major driver, and
it has already received orders from customers.
• SLT: 9M26 revenue doubled on a YoY basis. SL T demand used to focus on

one major customer, but management indicated that it has added AVGO and
MRVL as new customers. Moreover, demand in 4Q26/1H27 should be mostly
related to inference applications, including CPU/ASIC/LPU.
• Photonics: 9M26 revenue grew by 4x on  a YoY basis. Demand from
transceivers should remain solid and CPO should grow notably in 2027.
Chroma focuses on Insertion 3/4o and shipments for the mass production
line next year  are seen reaching 4-5x vs. that for the pilot line this year.

Moreover, it has added another customer for a pilot run in 9M27 and mass
production in 4Q27. At the industry level, if insertion is excluded from mass
production, Insertion 3 tool demand should be higher. Opportunities will
likely be much larger when CPO penetrates the scale-up network.
• New businesses: FT handlers have been qualified by multiple HPC/CSP
customers and orders have been placed. Burn-in ovens have secured the first
CSP customer, with more HPC customers expected to follow next year.

These should be new addressable markets for Chroma in the coming years.
Stay OW: We believe Chroma should continue to benefit from the AI compute/
networking/power testing opportunities due to its strong customer relationship,
solid execution and outstanding technology expertise. Our PT of NT$2,800 is based
on 40x 2027e P/E, implying a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

Test instrument and ATS segment looks set to reach another

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 4: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 5: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 6: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 40x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 40x is justified by the 68% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

### Page 1

EQUITY: FABLESS
MediaTek   2454.TW 2454 TT
Global Markets Research
30 October 2026
Dispels recent market noises with upside surprise; retain Buy
Quick Note
Maintain Buy and TP of TWD5,800
We recently noted that MediaTek’s earnings call could prove to be a positive catalyst for 
its share price, considering a number of unfounded concerns and noises had taken center 
stage before the call (Preview: market concerns overdone ). However, we believe some of 
MTK’s messages during earnings call turned out to be even more bullish than we had 
expected, including: (1) TPU v.8 contribution of USD2.5bn in 4Q26E (vs. previous 
guidance of more than USD2bn , and Street concerns around MTK potentially missing the 
USD2bn target); (2) management now expects ASIC sales in 2027E to reach the high end 
(USD16bn) of its prior guidance range (USD14-16bn) vs. our forecast of USD14bn; (3) 
TPU v.9 schedule is on track for 1Q28E mass production (a powerful clarification vs. 
Street expectation of 1-2 quarters’ delay); (4) management is very confident about v.9 
EMIB-T future yield trend after resolving 2-3 key issues (implying no CoWoS back-up plan, 
vs. Street expectation of a CoWoS-based v.9 project being opened); (5) Mediatek 
announced that it has secured a 2nd ASIC customer with sales contribution from 2028E; 
(6) ASIC-driven operating leverage will kick in from 4Q26E (guidance suggesting 19% 
OPM in 4Q26E, vs our estimate of 18.6% and 3Q26’s 16%; and management expects 
2027E OPM to be above 20%, vs our estimate of 22%). The only downside surprise might 
be its 4Q26E sales guidance, which suggests 25%-32% q-q growth vs. our estimate of 
34% q-q. We would watch if this is a low bar for MediaTek to beat. GPM guidance of 
45%+-1.5% is 1pp lower than previous quarter’s range (46%+-1.5%; and 46.3% actual in 
3Q26) due to ASIC — in line with our forecast of 45.4%. Overall, we expect the share 
price to react positively to the earnings call and, thus, reiterate our Buy rating and 
TWD5,800 TP, based on 25x unchanged 2027-28 EPS estimate. The stock is currently 
trading at 16.9x.

### Page 2

MediaTek: 3Q26 analysts briefing summary
Below comments are from MediaTek’s management, unless otherwise stated :
Results summary
3Q26 revenue was TWD168.1bn (+10.4% q-q, +18.3% y-y), above the high end of
the TWD152.2–159.8bn guidance.
The beat came mainly from better-than-expected customer demand and
improving supply.
○
Growth was driven by volume production of several new Smart Edge projects and
higher DRAM content in TV SoC.
○
•
GM was 46.3% (+0.1pp q-q, -0.2pp y-y), within the 46% ±1.5pp guidance.•

Opex ratio was 30.2% (vs 31.2% in 2Q26 and 30.9% in 3Q25), within the 31% ±2pp
guidance.
•
OPM was 16.1% (+1.1pp q-q, +0.5pp y-y).•
Net income was TWD28.2bn (+14.6% q-q, +10.8% y-y). Net margin was 16.8%
(+0.6pp q-q, -1.1pp y-y).
•
EPS was TWD17.50 (TWD15.28 in 2Q26, TWD15.84 in 3Q25). Non-TIFRS EPS was
TWD17.92.
•
Average FX was TWD31.99/USD (2Q26: 31.60; guidance assumption: 32).•
Prepared remarks
MediaTek completed a USD3.9bn overseas convertible bond offering in 3Q, with

participation from nVidia (NVDA US, Not rated) and Alphabet (GOOGL US, Not
rated).
This strengthens partnerships with key AI infrastructure players and helps secure
capacity for critical components.
○
•
MediaTek continues to make solid progress in both data center and edge devices.
Datacenter ASIC
Current gen: Demand remains very strong. Initial shipments began in
September, and MediaTek is confident of delivering USD2.5bn of revenue in
4Q26.
The 4Q upside comes from robust customer demand. MediaTek has

worked closely with supply chain partners to secure supply, including
substrates.
■
For 2027E, the program is expected to reach the high end of the revenue
target range, about USD16bn, or around 20% of the AI accelerator ASIC
market. This is underpinned by a solid supply outlook, and MediaTek is
working to secure additional capacity to meet demand.
■
•
Next gen: Development is progressing well and is on track for HVM in 1Q28.
The chip is purpose-built for the customer’s next-generation AI

infrastructure. It delivers multiple times higher performance per watt at a
significantly lower TCO.
■
It is expected to strengthen MediaTek’s market position and contribute
substantially to revenue growth.
■
•
Technology: Solid progress continues in 448G SerDes, 3.5D advanced
packaging, and CPO.
•
Broader customer base: MediaTek is expanding beyond silicon into system
and rack solutions. It connects custom xPUs to the NVLink Fusion ecosystem
and also supports UALink.

•
MediaTek continues to expand engagements with several customers.•
○
Edge
The RTX Spark AI PC (N1X), which integrates an nVidia GPU, became
commercially available in mid-October through major PC OEMs. Next-gen
SoC development is already underway.
•
Dimensity 9500 powers the latest Samsung Galaxy AI tablets. This is the third
consecutive generation of Samsung AI tablets using a MediaTek flagship
SoC.
•
○
•

Mobile: -1% q-q, -17% y-y (37% of revenue).
Flagship outperformed as customers launched new AI flagship smartphones,
including models with MediaTek’s first 2nm SoC, the Dimensity 9600.
○
•
Smart Edge: +22% q-q, +63% y-y (58% of revenue).
Y-y growth was driven by volume production of new projects: a 5G thin modem for
a new smartphone customer, new AI tablets, AI PCs, and automotive cockpits. TV

SoC revenue rose on higher DRAM content.
○
•

Y-y growth came mainly from share gains in data-center-related business.○

4Q26 guidance
Revenue is guided at TWD210.1–221.8bn (+25–32% q-q, +40–48% y-y), driven by
the AI accelerator ramp-up, despite seasonal softness in consumer electronics.
•
GM is guided at 45% ±1.5pp.
Datacenter business blended GM is still below the corporate average, but OPM
becomes accretive from 4Q.

○
Price adjustments for non-DC businesses reflect supply chain cost increases.○
•
Opex ratio is guided at 26% ±2pp.
4Q marks the starting point of OPM improvement. MediaTek expects 2027E OPM
of 20%+.
○
•
FX assumption is TWD31.7/USD.•
Full-year revenue growth is now expected to exceed the previous target of high-
single-digit growth in USD.
•
Q&A
Any changes in ASIC schedule and milestones?
The 2nd ASIC is on track and on schedule.
MediaTek is aware of market noise about the tape-out schedule. The chip has

multiple dies, and a couple have already taped out.
○
The large, more complex die will also tape out on schedule, though it takes about
three weeks longer than the others.
○
•
2028 will be another year of strong y-y growth based on the demand profile. No
details yet, as the supply chain is still being finalized.
•
EMIB-T confidence
MediaTek is working with the US IDM’s packaging process and has built a strong in-
house manufacturing team led by experienced fab veterans.

•
It is working closely with the IDM and substrate suppliers.•
Management has high confidence that yield will improve quickly once two or three key
problems are resolved.
MediaTek is working with both Taiwanese and Japanese suppliers.○
•
MediaTek is not dependent on a single supplier.
TSMC (2330 TT, Buy) remains its most important partner, and the two work
closely across the entire technology chain.
○
•
Next-next gen, TAM and contribution
MediaTek is working very closely with the customer on the next-next generation.•

The customer is aggressive in pushing performance up and TCO down.•
This will be another challenging chip, and the definition will take time to finalize.•
Management will share more when the time is right.•
Nomura | MediaTek 30 October 2026
3

### Page 4

New ASIC client
A 2nd customer is confirmed, separate from the lead customer.•
The project has kicked off, with first revenue and mass production expected in 2028.•
The customer is not from China.•
Vendor financing for LLM customers?
MediaTek will consider it if necessary and if it is the right customer.•
Returns versus capex have improved over the past six quarters. Management 
believes capex, whether from CSPs or frontier model companies, will generate 
enough revenue and cash flow. Some support may be needed during the transition 
period before cash generation stabilizes.
•
EMIB-T / HBM5 / technology capability and competition
The entry barrier is the ability to integrate multiple technologies into manufacturable, 
scalable products: advanced packaging, HBM5, 448G SerDes, and low power. It is 
not any single technology.
•
Competitors are strong, and MediaTek does not assume it is far ahead. However, it is 
confident it will win its share of the business.
•
NVLink Fusion benefits and customer interest
It is a business model with strong technical content, and it makes it much easier for 
customers to integrate their own designs.
•
The key advantage is much faster time to compute power.•
MediaTek and nVidia are in discussions with several potential customers. Feedback 
is positive, and MediaTek hopes to announce cases in the near term.
•
448G
448G SerDes has already taped out.•
448G is a must going forward. It is an N6 design with very stringent power 
consumption requirements.
•
It will not be on the critical path, and MediaTek will improve interoperability to serve a 
broader customer base.
•

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

### Page 1

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
3Q26 Preview: Deferrals Weigh on 3Q; ASIC Ramp Underpins 
4Q26E/2027E Growth
CITI'S TAKE 
Auras is scheduled to report 3Q26E results in mid-Nov, followed by its 
annual investor meeting in the following weeks. 3Q26 revenue fell short of 
mgmt. ’s guidance of at least +15% QoQ on GB300 demand deferrals and 
Thailand flooding. We expect GPM below our prior forecast on lower scale 
and front-loaded expenses, and forecast 3Q26 net profit of NT$1.24bn, 13% 
below cons. We now model 4Q26E revenue growth of +20% QoQ on 
deferred demand and the ASIC liquid cooling ramp from Nov, and 2027E 
revenue growth of +50% YoY as multiple ASIC projects broaden the growth 
base. We raise our TP to NT$2,000 (20x 2027E EPS). Reiterate Buy.
3Q26 preview — Auras reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.8bn (+13% QoQ/+64% YoY), 
below mgmt. ’s earlier guidance. The shortfall mainly reflected customers deferring 
GB300 demand into 4Q26 and the flooding in Thailand. Given lower revenue scale 
and certain expenses recognized ahead of the upcoming ramp, we expect 3Q26E 
GPM to come in at 28.2% (-0.2 ppt QoQ/-1.6 ppts YoY), below our prior forecast of 
30.6%. With the opex ratio largely in line, we forecast 3Q26 net profit of NT$1.24bn 
(+28% QoQ/+27% YoY), and EPS of NT$13.32, 13% below cons.
Multiple ASIC projects to support 4Q26E/2027E — Mgmt. guided for 4Q26E 
revenue growth of +20% QoQ, driven by deferred 3Q26 revenue and the mass 
production of the ASIC project’s liquid cooling version, which we expect from Nov. 
We raise our 4Q26E growth forecast to +20% QoQ (from +16%), with GPM improving 
to 31.1%. While VR cold plate qualification is ongoing, we believe Auras is well 
positioned to win multiple ASIC projects, with a second project likely to contribute 
from 2H27. We now forecast 2027E revenue growth of +45% YoY, with GPM 
expanding to 32.2% on a rising share of customized projects and a better mix.
Implications — We lower our 2026E earnings forecast by 5% on the softer 3Q26. We 
also raise our 2027E/28E earnings forecasts by 13%/20% on higher ASIC liquid 
cooling shipment assumptions and margin expansion. Our revised TP of NT$2,000 
(from NT$1,800) remains based on 20x 2027E EPS. We believe recent concerns over 
the timing of various AI server projects are largely priced in. Given Auras’ structural 
growth in the server business, widening liquid cooling content across GPU and ASIC 
platforms, and an optimizing global manufacturing footprint, we view current 
valuation as attractive and reiterate our Buy rating.

### Page 6

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
02 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Auras Technology
Company description
Auras Technology was founded in 1998 and focused on the design and
manufacturing of thermal modules for NB/PCs. It quickly became one of
Taiwan’s top two NB cooling component suppliers. Early products included
heatsinks, fins, heat pipes, and fans for NBs, PCs, and smartphones. As
device form factor evolved, the company invested in new technologies such

as ultra-thin vapor chamber in the 2000s to address the thermal challenges
of increasingly compact consumer electronics. During the 2010s, Auras
expanded into graphic card and server cooling and was an early adopter of
liquid cooling technology. By 2012, the company had begun developing liquid
cooling component and was among the first Taiwanese firms to venture into
AI server liquid cooling.
Investment strategy
We rate Auras a Buy. Auras is emerging as a key liquid-cooling supplier as

hyperscalers expand deployment. The company has secured qualifications for
cold plates and manifolds, opening access to a wider slate of Nvidia and CSP
projects. Thailand-based production offers non-China capacity at a scale
aligned with hyperscaler sourcing plans, allowing Auras to compete more
effectively for next-generation products.
Valuation
We value Auras using a PE-based approach, reflecting its accelerating
earnings trajectory and rising share in liquid cooling market of AI servers. Our

TP of NT$2,000 is based on 20x 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified
given the company’s expanding server revenue base, deeper engagement
with CSP customers, and broader product portfolio across cold plates, CDMs,
and CDUs.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model, but assigning a
High Risk rating is not supported in our view by other qualitative factors such
as rapid RVL progress and Thailand expansion, and so a High Risk rating has

not been applied. Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target
price include: 1) slower adoption of liquid cooling; 2) heightened price
competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling components;
and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We update our view on Advantech after 3Q26 results 
(released on 4 November).

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat. Advantech’s 3Q26 earnings of 
TWD6.2bn (EPS: TWD7.15) beat our estimate and BBG consensus by 
26% and 28%, respectively, driven by record-high OPM (20.9%) and better-
than-expected non-ops (gains from its Japan plant’s asset disposal). 
However, GM fell 1pp QoQ to 36.7%, mainly due to raw-material cost 
inflation. Management said CPU, memory and other component costs rose 
to 70% (vs. 30% previously). Although the company could pass on higher 
costs, it couldn’t maintain its blended GM on an incremental sales.

Business update. Looking ahead, Advantech remains positive on its 4Q26 
and 2027 outlook, supported by a c.USD2.5bn order backlog and a 3Q26 
B/B ratio of 1.64 (vs. 1.6 in October). Management said cost-inflation-driven 
demand pull-ins were limited in 9M26, but order visibility has extended as 
clients seek greater allocations. Advantech sees AI infrastructure, 
semiconductors, factory automation and medical equipment as key growth 
drivers, with shipments in these applications expected to grow c.15% YoY 
in 2026E and into 2027E, based on our forecasts. Edge AI remains its 
fastest-growing application: its revenue contribution rose to 24.5% in 9M26, 
with c.109% YoY growth, vs. 22.5% in 1H26 and the company’s c.30% 
target for 2026. Growth has been driven mainly by higher ASPs, but we 
expect unit growth to become the primary driver in 2027E as ASPs lap the 
increase in 2026 already. In the near term, while management guides its 
revenue would decrease by single-digit % QoQ due to fewer working days 
over Christmas holiday period in 4Q26, but we believe this is too 
conservative. We expect 4Q26E revenue to be broadly flat QoQ, as a solid 
backlog is on hand. We also expect gross margin to remain similar to 3Q26 
amid ongoing cost inflation. Management reported ASP increases of 10-
20% for CPUs and 15-20% for PCBs, while SSD and memory prices have 
been relatively stable recently. We expect shortages to ease gradually, 
particularly for memory, but to persist for CPUs and CCLs.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 7-22% to reflect 
Advantech’s 3Q26 results. We reaffirm our Outperform (2) call and raise 
our 12M TP to TWD750 (from TWD700), based on a lower PER of 26x 
(prior: 30x; slightly higher than the mid-point of its past-3-year range of 20-
37x), as we are closer to the end of the up-cycle, and applied to our 1-year 
forward EPS. Key downside risk: slower-than-expected demand recovery.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 10-17% above the consensus, 
likely due to our more bullish outlook on Advantech’s revenue.

4 November 2026

3Q26 results: positive tone towards 2027

➢ Up-cycle will last into 2027, with limited early pull-in shipments in 2026 
➢ Key growth drivers in 2026 include ASP hike and fast Edge AI growth 
➢ Reaffirming our Outperform (2) rating; raising 12-month TP to TWD750

Company profile 
Founded in 1981 and listed in 1999, Advantech is the largest industrial PC (IPC) maker worldwide in 
terms of embedded board/system sales, followed by the company’s main competitors, Germany-based 
Kontron and US-based Radisys. The company mainly provides embedded computing boards, chassis, 
industrial computers, applied computers and design-in services. The businesses are categorised into 4 
segments: Embedded design-in, Industrial IoT, Service IoT, and Global services.

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

Document 277: AI抛售潮中买家消失！高盛交易台电话被“打爆”却无人接盘，对冲基金、散户齐撤

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI抛售潮中买家消失！高盛交易台电话被“打爆”却无人接盘，对冲基金、散户齐撤.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛交易员称，周三是该⾏股票交易台全年最繁忙交易⽇之⼀，周四完全相反，投资者纷
纷询问市场还可能跌多深，却⼏乎看不到买盘进场；部分对冲基⾦开始卖出周三刚刚买⼊
的股票。瑞银数据显⽰，周四抛压主要来⾃对冲基⾦。以摩根⼤通数据估算，散⼾净卖出
约 13 亿美元，单⽇规模创⼀年新⾼。 “ 恐慌指数 ”VIX 盘中飙升⾄⼋个⽉⾼位。投资者抢购保
护性期权，主要股指看跌期权权利⾦为通常两倍。
摘要：
美东时间 11 ⽉ 5 ⽇周四，美股科技股遭遇猛烈抛售之际，⾼盛集团的股票交易台电话⼏乎被 “ 打
爆 ” ，投资者纷纷打来询问市场还可能跌多深，但真正愿意逢低买⼊的资⾦却寥寥⽆⼏。
⾼盛交易员 John Flood 在发给客⼾的市场评论中形容，周四的交易环境与前⼀⽇截然相反：周
三是该⾏股票交易台全年最繁忙的交易⽇之⼀，⽽周四则 “ 完全相反 ” ，交易员接到⼤量询问市场
情况的电话，却⼏乎看不到买盘进场。
这番描述折射出 AI 交易突然降温后，市场情绪从追涨迅速转向避险。纳斯达克综合指数周四收跌
5.52% ，费城半导体指数暴跌超过 12% ，科技股整体市值蒸发约 2.5 万亿美元。与此同时，波动

率飙升、散⼾集中卖出、对冲基⾦削减仓位，进⼀步放⼤了市场跌势。
⾼盛交易员眼中的市场反转：周三积极买⼊，周四急于撤退
Flood 表⽰，周三市场上涨后，部分此前在美国中期选举前⼤幅降低⻛险敞⼝的投资者重新⼊
场，买回了⼀些股票。但周四市场突然逆转，部分对冲基⾦⼜开始卖出前⼀⽇刚刚买⼊的股
票，尤其是⼤型科技股和超⼤市值科技公司。
他指出，⻓期投资机构再度陷⼊观望，市场尚未出现愿意出⼿托底的买家。与此同时，⾯向散
⼾客⼾的券商也反映，卖盘连续第⼆天明显多于买盘。
交易活跃度同样出现异常。 Flood 称，截⾄周四盘中，标普 500 指数 ETF SPY 成交量较过去 20 个
交易⽇均值⾼出约 160% ，追踪纳斯达克 100 指数的 QQQ 成交量⾼出约 100% ，半导体 ETF SMH
成交量⾼出约 150% ， DRAM 相关股票成交量则⾼出约 75% 。
成交量激增反映出投资者正在密集调整仓位，但并不意味着所有交易都是净卖出。结合交易员
所描述的买盘缺位，市场更明显的信号是：在股价快速下跌时，原本可能⼊场承接的资⾦选择

了等待。
机构抛压集中释放，散⼾单⽇净卖出规模创近⼀年新⾼
⾼盛交易台观察到的买盘退缩，也得到了其他机构交易数据的印证。
据路透社报道，瑞银交易员数据显⽰，周四该⾏机构客⼾的主动交易流向明显偏向卖出，抛压
主要来⾃对冲基⾦，科技股尤其是存储芯⽚和半导体板块遭遇集中抛售。瑞银交易员将新兴云
服务商和半导体股票的⾛势形容为明显的 “ 投降式抛售 ” 。
⾼盛交易员也指出，周四对冲基⾦正在卖出前⼀⽇重新买⼊的部分⼤型科技股。市场不仅出现
板块内部轮动，更有投资者直接削减整体⻛险敞⼝。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

散⼾投资者同样加⼊撤退⾏列。 Vanda Research 的数据显⽰，周四散⼾投资者单只股票净卖出
规模有望创下疫情以来最⼤单⽇纪录。摩根⼤通截⾄美东时间上午 11 时 30 分的数据则显⽰，若
将 ETF 计⼊，散⼾净卖出规模达到约 13 亿美元，为过去⼀年最⾼。
更值得注意的是，散⼾通常会在市场下跌时买⼊股票 “ 抄底 ” ，但这⼀次，个股市场的逢低买盘明
显减弱。 Vanda Research 策略师指出，传统的下跌⾏情中散⼾往往会买⼊回调，但周四这种买
盘实际上已经从个股中消失。
VIX 盘中飙升⾄⼋个⽉⾼位，投资者抢购保护性期权
股票抛售之外，投资者对冲市场⻛险的需求也迅速升温。
衡量市场波动的 “ 恐慌指数 ”—— 芝加哥期权交易所波动率指数（ VIX ）周四盘中⼀度上涨 11.75
点，升⾄ 28.36 ，为⼋个⽉⾼位，创 2025 年 4 ⽉美国对等关税冲击以来最⼤单⽇盘中涨幅；收盘
报 28.18 。
期权市场的变化同样显⽰，投资者正在为进⼀步下跌购买保护。路透社援引期权分析机构

Mercury Alerts 的数据称，周四主要股指产品的看跌期权权利⾦约为通常⽔平的两倍，反映出市
场对下⾏保护的需求显著增加。
互动券商⾸席策略师 Steve Sosnick 表⽰，市场情绪在短短⼀天内发⽣了明显变化：周三投资者
还认为没有必要购买保护，周四却突然意识到需要对冲⻛险。
此外，杠杆和反向 ETF 以及部分量化策略通常会在临近收盘时根据市场波动调整仓位，这可能进
⼀步放⼤尾盘涨跌。野村证券盘中估算，杠杆 ETF 周四收盘前可能需要卖出约 470 亿美元股票，
这将是该机构⾃ 2006 年开始追踪相关数据以来第⼆⼤规模的此类卖盘，且卖出压⼒主要集中在
科技和 AI 相关基⾦。
这意味着，部分卖盘并⾮完全来⾃投资者主动改变⻓期判断，也可能是市场下跌触发了策略性
减仓，进⽽形成进⼀步的下跌压⼒。
数据中⼼建设与 AI 投资回报遭质疑，抛售从芯⽚蔓延⾄产业链
本轮抛售的直接背景，是 AI 相关股票此前累积了⼤量资⾦和较⾼市场预期，⽽美国中期选举结果
⼜令数据中⼼建设前景蒙上政策阴影。

⽬采取更严格的审批、能源和环保要求，从⽽拖慢美国 AI 基础设施建设。半导体、存储芯⽚、新
兴云服务商以及数据中⼼电⼒供应商因此受到冲击。
⾼盛交易员 Flood 还提出了⼀个更具体的担忧：到 2028 年，市场可能⾯临数据中⼼⼟地、电⼒和
建筑容量不⾜的问题。如果数据中⼼基础设施建设速度跟不上芯⽚交付，市场就可能从担忧芯
⽚供应不⾜，转向担忧芯⽚过剩、缺少⾜够的数据中⼼承接。
这也解释了为何周四的抛售并不局限于芯⽚制造商。数据中⼼建设承包商、电⼒供应商、新兴
云服务商等板块也遭遇明显卖压。投资者开始重新审视 AI 产业链的扩张节奏，以及巨额算⼒投资
能否按预期转化为实际收⼊和利润。
与此同时，近期⾼通和 Arm 的业绩展望未能满⾜市场预期，也进⼀步削弱了投资者对半导体板块
的信⼼。 AI 需求⻓期增⻓的逻辑并未因此消失，但市场对增⻓兑现速度、资本开⽀回报以及产业
链各环节盈利能⼒的要求正在提⾼。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
对于市场⽽⾔，关键问题已经从 “AI 投资还会增⻓多少 ” ，转向 “ 这些投资能否按时建成、获得⾜
够电⼒，并产⽣⾜以⽀撑当前估值的回报 ” 。当投资者同时选择减仓、对冲和等待买⼊机会时，
交易员电话被打爆，却看不到买家进场，也就成为这轮 AI 抛售中最直观的市场情绪写照。

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by a continued global selloff in tech
stocks, leading to safe haven flows into US Treasuries, Japanese Go -
vernment bonds and German bunds. As a result, their yields fell des -
pite energy prices surging to or past their Spring highs. Economic data
generally showed continued resilience. French bonds were under rising

pressure, but Moody's unexpected decision to maintain its France ra -
ting unchanged could, at the margin, help stabilise the spread. The Fed,
ECB and BOJ should be on hold next week while signaling further rate
action is likely.
EUROPEAN UNION
A win for securitization. The Council and the European Parliament have
reached a provisional political agreement on measures to revive and
simplify the EU’s securitization market. The updated framework will

reduce banks’ capital requirements for the safest and most well-struc-
tured transactions, notably for a newly introduced “resilient” category
of securitization. As a key component of the “One Europe, One Market”
initiative and the EU’s Savings and Investment Union agenda, this aims
to channel increased lending to households and businesses. The rele -
vant legal texts must now be finalised.
EUROZONE
Expansion continues. The flash PMIs weakened in October, mainly on

the back of a decline in France. The EZ composite index dipped to 51.9
(-1.2 m/m), dragged down by services (51.7, -1.3 m/m), although ma -
nufacturing also dipped (52.4, -0.5 m/m). Both remain in expansion
territory, and manufacturing new export orders rose to their highest
level since early 2022. Price signals were also rather reassuring, with
the services output price index easing to 54.9 (-1.7 m/m). Households
are more cautious: consumer confidence fell for a second consecutive

month to a five-month low (-17.9). Households’ inflation expectations
edged up at short horizons (+0.1 pp to 3.6% at one year and 3.1% at
three years), but long-term expectations remained anchored, with the
five-year median stable at 2.5%.
France
Rising market stress despite decent economic news; a reprieve from
Moody’s. The spread on French 10-year bond relative to Germany’s
peaked to 174 bps on Friday, the highest since 2011 and only 16 bps
short of its all-time high, as markets price in anticipation of worst-case

scenarios regarding the country’s ability to repair its public finances.
The 10Y yield briefly exceeded 5.1% before falling back to 5% (above
the US equivalent). Nonetheless, services output was stable in August,
following a 0.6% m/m increase in July, a much higher pace than in Q2.
The rebound is driven by the catering sector, the digital sector, business
services, and real estate. The INSEE business climate improved from
96 to 97 (long-term average = 100) in October, but the survey was

conducted before the riots. The main improvement was in manufactu-
ring, which rose from 101 to 105, driven by increased new orders in the
aeronautics sector, and to a lesser extent by the services sector (from
98 to 99). The PMI survey (conducted partially after the riots) was
less positive: the composite index fell to 48.4 (-2.7 m/m), with a sharp
decline in services (48.4; -2.8 m/m) and a slightly smaller decline in
manufacturing (49.3; -1.3 m/m). Moody's unexpectedly announced af -

ter the market close on Friday that it was maintaining France’s ra -
ting at Aa3 on account of France’s diversified economic structure and
sound public institutions, but maintained its negative outlook. This is
on par with the UK and one notch above S&P and Fitch. Moody’s made
clear keeping this rating is conditional on a budget being adopted that
reduces the fiscal deficit, along with an easing of social and market
stress going forward. Saturday’s demonstrations across the country

saw strong attendance but only limited violent incidents.
Germany
Prices continue to rise, but confidence surveys remain on an upward
trend. Producer prices rose 6.7% y/y in September (+2.1pp m/m; the
highest level in 3.5 years) due to higher prices for energy and inter -
mediate goods, primarily metals. The ZEW Economic Sentiment Index
improved beyond consensus forecasts in October and returned to its
highest level since May 2023 (-35.9; +11.2 m/m), albeit the Economic

Expectations Index fell to its lowest level since July (28.1; -6.6 m/m).
The recovery in economic activity continues, although a slowdown is
evident: the flash Composite PMI slowed (52.8; -1 m/m; the lowest in
2 months) due to weaker growth in the services sector (51.7; -1.1 m/m;
the lowest in 2 months) and in manufacturing output (55.1; -0.9 m/m;
the lowest in 3 months). Nevertheless, in both sectors, hiring is rising
slightly, supported by growth in export sales and the spillover effects of

investment plans. Despite persistently high input prices, selling prices
are slowing (reaching their lowest level since February in the manu -
facturing sector).
UNITED KINGDOM
Inflation, wage growth and activity accelerate. CPI inflation overshot
consensus on energy prices, printing at 0.4% m/m and 3.5% y/y in Sep-
tember (consensus: 3.4%, prior: 3.1%), its highest in 11 months. Core
CPI came in at 2.8% y/y (up 0.2 pp), in line with consensus. Pressures

remained persistent according to the PPI at 8.3% y/y (prior: 6.7%). La -
bour market and activity data also surprised positively. Payroll growth
reached 102k in August (3m/3m), bringing the unemployment rate to
4.7% (down 0.2 pp), its lowest since July 2025. October PMI surveys
beat expectations in both manufacturing (52.8, consensus: 51.8, prior:
51.9) and services (53.1, consensus: 51.8, prior: 52.1). The only negative
news came from headline retail sales, falling 0.4% m/m in September,

with the core gauge (excluding petrol) deteriorating by -0.7% m/m.
UNITED STATES
Construction struggling, GENIUS Act implemented. Housing starts
dropped -5.2% m/m in September (-7.4% y/y). This third consecutive
monthly decline dragged the Q3 average to its lowest since Q2 2020.
Building permits also came in negative, at -0.2% m/m (-3.0% y/y), while
the NAHB homebuilder sentiment index plunged to its weakest since
2012. The average 30-year fixed mortgage rate eased to 7.32% (still

134bp above pre-war level), as the ongoing AI selloff pushed down
US treasury yields. The GENIUS Act final implementation rule from the
Office of the Comptroller of the Currency’s (OCC) leaves the door open
for stablecoin customers to receive yield or rewards. Issuers remain
prohibited from paying holders any form of interest or yield solely
in connection with the holding, use, or retention of payment stable -
coins. This represents a shift from the OCC’s February proposal and

has renewed concerns within the banking industry about deposit flight.
The rule will take effect on January 18, 2027.
JAPAN
Activity and inflation keep rising. Composite PMI was unchanged in
September at 52.3, solidly in expansion territory, as an improvement
in services activity offset slower output growth in manufacturing. The
Manufacturing PMI disappointed at 53.3 (-0.8 m/m; an 8-month low).
Underlying price pressures, meanwhile, extended. Headline CPI was

with consensus. More importantly, the New Core index, which strips
